I'll sit there on my lunch break at the store, looking at food I used to actually like, and I just end up throwing most of it in the trash. I mean, I used to get really excited about things outside of work. Like going to gallery nights, playing basketball, grabbing food-truck tacos, or even just helping kids out with their art stuff. But lately... I don't know. I'll go, but I just stand there feeling completely flat. I usually end up making an excuse to leave early because I'm just not into it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two months?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say I feel sad or depressed. I'm not walking around crying or feeling super down or anything. It's more just... like I said, I just feel flat. Just kind of blank most of the day, every day. But I wouldn't call it feeling depressed, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don't enjoy things as much as you used to, even things you really liked doing before?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I kind of just mentioned that. It's like I was saying with the gallery nights and playing basketball. I still go sometimes, but I just don't get any enjoyment out of it anymore. The things I used to really like doing just don't do anything for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep, energy levels, or how you feel about yourself during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has definitely been off. Most nights I just lie there in bed... I keep replaying this award thing in my head, and different conversations from the store. I just don't have the appetite for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any weight loss recently because of not eating as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: I haven't really stepped on a scale, so I don't know for sure about the weight. But yeah, like I was just saying, that's really what made me come in here. I just barely want to eat anymore. I'll sit there on my lunch break at the store, looking at food I used to actually like, and I just end up throwing most of it in the trash. So I probably have lost some weight from doing that, I just mostly notice that my appetite is completely gone.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about your appetite and eating habits?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two months now. I guess it started right around the time I won this window display award at work, and the district manager started paying a lot of attention to me. So yeah, about two months of just not wanting to eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you usually enjoy most days, even outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I mean, I used to get really excited about things outside of work. Like going to gallery nights, playing basketball, grabbing food-truck tacos, or even just helping kids out with their art stuff. But lately... I don't know. I'll go, but I just stand there feeling completely flat. I usually end up making an excuse to leave early because I'm just not into it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two months?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say I feel sad or depressed. I'm not walking around crying or feeling super down or anything. It's more just... like I said, I just feel flat. Just kind of blank most of the day, every day. But I wouldn't call it feeling depressed, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don't enjoy things as much as you used to, even things you really liked doing before?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I kind of just mentioned that. It's like I was saying with the gallery nights and playing basketball. I still go sometimes, but I just don't get any enjoyment out of it anymore. The things I used to really like doing just don't do anything for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep, energy levels, or how you feel about yourself during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has definitely been off. Most nights I just lie there in bed... I keep replaying this award thing in my head, and different conversations from the store. And then, if I actually do manage to fall asleep, I end up waking up way too early. Once I'm up, I just can't get my mind to shut back off. So I guess I'm pretty tired during the day just from not sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant loss of energy or increased fatigue during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, I know I just said I'm tired from not sleeping, but it's kind of a physical thing, too. By the middle of a shift at work, my body just feels really heavy. Like, even just doing the basic stuff, um, straightening racks or walking back to the stockroom... it just takes way more out of me than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn't worth living or thought about harming yourself during this time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any thoughts like that. I mean, I'm definitely tired and things feel pretty pointless right now, but I haven't wanted to hurt myself or anything. I don't think life isn't worth living, it's just... hard to get through the days lately. But no, no thoughts of harming myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not good enough or blamed yourself for things more than usual during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Um, I keep thinking about this award I got at work... and the chance to be a supervisor. I just, I keep thinking I don't deserve it. Like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I'm doing a good job, even though people tell me I didn't. I just feel really guilty about it, like I'm a fraud or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual or that your thoughts feel slowed down lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. And my thoughts don't feel slowed down, either. They seem to be going at a normal speed, just... mostly focused on the same negative stuff. But no, no real slowness.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in the way you feel or act that you think might be related to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I can't really think of any other changes in how I'm acting or feeling right now. Um, at work I keep losing my place on really simple stuff. Like, if I'm counting inventory or trying to pick colors for a display... I just lose my focus. And I end up second-guessing myself over things I used to do fast without even thinking about it. So yeah, making decisions and just staying on track has definitely been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most of the time, you're in a down or low mood, even if you're not exactly feeling sad or crying?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I wouldn't say I feel down or low most of the time. I mean, I'm just tired, you know? And I get stuck feeling bad about messing up at work, like I mentioned.
